# Reading level: sentences above grade 8 (warning only), 2026-11-21

Generated from `npm run test:published-library` on branch content/approved-legal-changes-2026-11-21, after the approved legal changes of 2026-11-21. Since the site owner's decision of 2026-11-21 the grade 8 reading level is a warning, never a failure; word-for-word rule and statute quotes (the text in quotation marks after a citation) are not scored; no legal wording was changed to lower a score. Each sentence is scored on its own (Flesch-Kincaid, with citations replaced by a one-syllable word), so a short sentence with one long word can score high.

2847 sentences in the published answers. (The rules-only pages are the rules' own quotes, which are not scored, plus their deadline text, which no reading check covers.)

| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What is happening | 15.5 | Ontario has a Director of the Family Responsibility Office. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What is happening | 9.4 | The Director's job is to enforce support orders filed in the Director's office. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What is happening | 8.3 | The clerk or registrar of the court that made a support order must file it there promptly once it is signed. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 8.5 | While a support order is filed there, only the Director may enforce it. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 9.3 | The one exception is someone given the Director's powers under section 4 of the Act. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 10.4 | They also include when the person owed support has not met reasonable requests for facts the Director needs. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 9.8 | A support order and any support deduction order that goes with it must be filed again together. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 11.7 | Whoever files or refiles a support order there must give notice immediately. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 12.3 | It can also be sent online, through Justice Services Online or the Ontario Courts Public Portal, whichever is available. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 11.7 | A payment order is a temporary or final order to pay money to another person, such as a support order. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 12.3 | While a support order is filed in the Director's office, only the Director may use them, apart from the exception above. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 10.4 | A support deduction order can be enforced only by the Director, whether or not it is filed there. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 10.1 | The ways include a request for a financial statement, seizure and sale, and garnishment. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What to do next | 10.4 | When the Director enforces an order, the rules for the person the order favours apply to the Director. |
| family:both:support-order-not-paid | What happens after | 10.5 | But the Director may keep enforcing to collect arrears owed to an agency from a past assignment. |

| civil:plaintiff:defendant-noted-in-default | What is happening | 9.1 | The registrar has noted the defendant in default, and no judgment has been signed or granted yet. |

| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 8.4 | Next comes a copy of any order about the trial, including an order made under Rule 6.1. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 8.6 | It states that the record holds the required documents and that the time to deliver pleadings has run out. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 9.3 | Where it applies, it also states that a defendant who did not deliver a defence has been noted in default. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 12.9 | Where it applies, it also states that judgment was obtained against a defendant, or that the action was discontinued or dismissed against one. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 8.6 | These are any notice of special damages, or any order about the trial, made after you filed the record. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 10.9 | They also include any order under rule 50.07 and the pre-trial conference report under rule 50.08. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 11.3 | In an undefended action, they include any affidavit to be used as evidence. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 9.9 | The way of setting down is different for an action under the simplified procedure (Rule 76). |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 9.6 | There, you must set it down within 180 days after the first statement of defence or notice of intent to defend is filed. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What to do next | 13.3 | This is done by serving a notice of readiness for pre-trial conference (Form 76C) on every party to the action and to any counterclaim, crossclaim or third party claim. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 8.4 | This is not an automatic dismissal. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.1 | Once you set the action down, you cannot start or continue discovery or motions without leave. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 16.9 | If your action is covered by mandatory mediation, one party must first file with the mediation co-ordinator the notice naming the mediator and date (Form 24.1A), or the mediator's report. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 8.4 | If your action is under the simplified procedure, it is set down another way. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 13.2 | That is by a notice of readiness for pre-trial conference (Form 76C), within 180 days after the first statement of defence or notice of intent to defend is filed. |

| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 8.6 | You have 180 days after the day the first statement of defence or notice of intent to defend was filed. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.6 | This applies only to an action under the simplified procedure. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.3 | If that day was February 29, the anniversary is February 28, except in a leap year. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 10.7 | It does not apply if a party files a timetable signed by all parties, and a draft order, at least 30 days before the time ends. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.3 | The timetable must give a date, no more than two years after the time ends, by which the action will be set down or restored. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.5 | If the parties do not agree on a timetable, any party can ask for a status hearing before the time ends. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 9.8 | If a time counted forward ends on a Saturday, Sunday or holiday, it ends on the next day that is not a holiday (r. 3.01 (1) (c)). |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | Your deadline | 10.2 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the safe course is to act by the last day before it that is not a holiday. |

| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What happens after | 11.1 | Setting down does not end your duty to keep the undertakings you gave on an examination for discovery. |
| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What happens after | 8.2 | Duties under some other rules also still apply. |

| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What happens after | 11.8 | Unless the court orders otherwise, the parties must book a pre-trial conference with the registrar within 180 days after the action is set down. |

| civil:plaintiff:ready-to-set-down | What happens after | 12 | Within 60 days after the action is set down, the parties must also agree on dates for serving experts' reports, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What is happening | 9.1 | The rules call a person who is entitled to enforce an order for money the creditor. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What is happening | 8.4 | Besides any other method the law provides, an order for money may be enforced in four ways. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What is happening | 10.5 | They are a writ of seizure and sale (Form 60A), garnishment, a writ of sequestration (Form 60B), and the appointment of a receiver. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.2 | To get a writ of seizure and sale, file a requisition with the registrar where the case was started. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 8.5 | The requisition says how much is owing and the rate of postjudgment interest. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.9 | A lawyer or a person licensed by the Law Society may instead ask for the writ through the Teranet software. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.3 | If the order is for payment at or after a set future time, the writ cannot be issued until that time has passed. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.2 | You may then file a direction to enforce (Form 60F) with the sheriff, with a copy of the order as entered. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.1 | To use it, file a requisition for garnishment (Form 60G) with the registrar where the case was started. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.1 | Include a copy of the order as entered, and an affidavit. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 11.2 | The affidavit must state several things the rule lists, such as the amount owing with postjudgment interest. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 8.8 | It must also name each person the notice is to go to, and say why you believe they owe or will owe the debtor money. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 10.5 | The registrar then issues a notice of garnishment (Form 60H) for each person named, who is called a garnishee. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 11.9 | It must be served by ordinary mail, by personal service, or by an alternative to personal service. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 9.1 | If the garnishee is a financial institution, serve it at the branch where the debt is payable. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 10.7 | If the debtor is a corporation, an officer or director may be examined for it. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 14 | For a partnership or sole proprietorship, a partner or sole proprietor whom the order can be enforced against may be examined. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 10.8 | Only one such examination of a debtor may be held in a 12-month period in the same case, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 13.7 | The debtor must be served with a notice of examination personally or by an alternative to personal service. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 11.1 | A person examined on the debtor's behalf must be served with a notice of examination in one of two ways. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What to do next | 10.5 | Or it may be served on that person personally, and not by an alternative to personal service. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 9.3 | If that day was February 29, the anniversary is February 28, except in a leap year. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 8.4 | A writ filed with a sheriff is renewed by filing a request to renew (Form 60E) with the sheriff. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 8.4 | A renewal can be filed only in the six years before the expiry it relates to. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 8.4 | A notice of garnishment also lasts six years and can be renewed before it expires. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 9.8 | If a time counted forward ends on a Saturday, Sunday or holiday, it ends on the next day that is not a holiday (r. 3.01 (1) (c)). |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | Your deadline | 10.2 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the safe course is to act by the last day before it that is not a holiday. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 10.9 | A garnishee who admits owing the debtor money must pay it to the sheriff as the notice sets out, subject to section 7 of the Wages Act. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 10.6 | Within 10 days after being served, such a garnishee must serve you and the debtor with a garnishee's statement (Form 60I), and file it. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 11.3 | If a notice of appeal is delivered, it stays (pauses) the parts of the order for paying money until the appeal is decided. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 12.2 | While an order is stayed, no steps may be taken under it or to enforce it, except as a judge of the appeal court orders or as the rules allow. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 9.8 | A writ of execution can still be issued and filed in a sheriff's office or land registry office. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 10 | But no instruction or direction to enforce the writ may be given to a sheriff while the stay remains in effect. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 10.5 | An appeal from an order refusing to set aside a default judgment does not stay that judgment, but it may be stayed by order. |
| civil:plaintiff:judgment-unpaid | What happens after | 9.8 | You are entitled to the costs of your enforcement steps, unless the court orders otherwise on a motion. |
| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What is happening | 9.6 | Discovery has not started. |

| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What is happening | 12.3 | Second, every defendant whose time to deliver a defence has run out without delivering one has been noted in default. |
| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What to do next | 8.6 | Second, that person is or may be liable to you for a separate claim for damages or other relief. |
| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What to do next | 11.3 | If you bring a third party claim, it must be issued within 10 days after you deliver your defence, or at any time before you are noted in default. |

| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What to do next | 10.7 | Serve the third party personally or by an alternative to personal service. |
| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What to do next | 9.5 | You must also serve it on every other party in the same time, but personal service is not needed for them. |

| civil:defendant:defence-delivered | What to do next | 13.2 | A statement of defence and crossclaim must be delivered within the time for delivering your defence, or at any time before you are noted in default. |

| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What is happening | 8.4 | In the rules, you are the debtor, and the person entitled to enforce it is the creditor. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What is happening | 10.5 | They are a writ of seizure and sale (Form 60A), garnishment, a writ of sequestration (Form 60B), and appointing a receiver. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What is happening | 8.4 | A writ of sequestration may be issued only with the court's leave, obtained on a motion. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What is happening | 10.9 | The court may grant leave only if it is satisfied that other ways of enforcing the order are not working, or are not expected to work. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 10.5 | The creditor must serve the notice of garnishment on you, with a copy of the creditor's affidavit. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 8.4 | Each notice names one garnishee, a person the creditor believes owes or will owe you money. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 14.1 | A copy of your notice of motion must be served on the sheriff by ordinary mail, by personal service, or by an alternative to personal service. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 12.9 | Once the sheriff gets it, the sheriff must suspend (pause) payments to the creditor under the notice until the motion is decided or abandoned. |
| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 12.2 | The creditor may also examine you about things such as why the order is not paid, your income and property, and the debts you owe and that others owe you. |

| civil:defendant:judgment-being-enforced | What to do next | 8.4 | That applies before a writ of seizure and sale or a notice of garnishment is issued. |

| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What is happening | 10.6 | A motion for summary judgment asks the court to decide all or part of the claim on affidavits or other evidence. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What is happening | 9.5 | The court must grant summary judgment if it is satisfied there is no genuine issue that needs a trial on a claim or defence. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What is happening | 10.9 | It must also grant it if the parties agree to have all or part of the claim decided this way, and the court is satisfied that is appropriate. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 10.2 | You must set out specific facts, in affidavits or other evidence, that show there is a genuine issue that needs a trial. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 10.2 | An affidavit on this motion may state what the person swearing it was told and believes, if it names the source and says that they believe it. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 13.6 | But at the hearing, the court may, if appropriate, draw an adverse inference when a party does not give evidence from a person who has personal knowledge of facts in dispute. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 11.5 | On this motion, each party must serve a factum on every other party to the motion, and it must meet the requirements of rule 4.06.1. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 12.3 | If you are responding, you must serve your factum and file it with proof of service at the court office where the motion will be heard, at least four days before the hearing. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 13.6 | If you brought the motion, then unless the court orders otherwise, you must also serve a motion record on every other party at least seven days before the hearing. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 12.9 | If you are responding, the moving party's motion record holds the affidavits and other material any party has served for use on the motion. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 8.1 | The court file is not put before the judge unless the judge asks for it or a party requisitions it. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 11.8 | If material you will use on the motion is not in the moving party's motion record, you may put it in a responding party's motion record. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 12.1 | You serve it on every other party and file it, with proof of service, in the court office where the motion will be heard, at least four days before the hearing. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | What to do next | 9.7 | It holds a table of contents and a copy of any material you will use on the motion that is not in the motion record. |
| civil:both:summary-judgment-motion | Your deadline | 11.3 | If you are bringing the motion for summary judgment, serve the notice of motion, motion record and your factum on the other parties. |

| family:both:final-order-made | What is happening | 11.1 | Rule 38 of the Family Law Rules covers appeals from the Ontario Court of Justice to the Superior Court of Justice. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What is happening | 10.2 | It applies to appeals under the laws it lists, such as section 48 of the Family Law Act and section 73 of the Children's Law Reform Act. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What is happening | 9.8 | A final order of a Superior Court judge is appealed to the Divisional Court or the Court of Appeal. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What is happening | 11.7 | For example, a final order of a Family Court judge made only under an Ontario law goes to the Divisional Court. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What is happening | 12.4 | For appeals to the Divisional Court or the Court of Appeal, rules 61, 62 and 63 of the Rules of Civil Procedure apply, with necessary changes. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 11.3 | To appeal a final order of the Ontario Court of Justice, you must serve a notice of appeal (Form 38) within 30 days after the date of the order. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 11.3 | Serve it on every other party affected by the appeal or entitled to appeal, and on the clerk of the court where the order was made. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 12.3 | For those, also serve every other person entitled to notice under subsection 79 (3) of that Act who appeared at the hearing. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.3 | If you serve it but do not file it in those 10 days, the appeal is treated as withdrawn, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 14.4 | To appeal to the Divisional Court or the Court of Appeal, you must serve a notice of appeal within 30 days after the order was made, unless a statute or the rules say otherwise. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 14.8 | Serve it on every party whose interest may be affected by the appeal, subject to the exceptions in rule 61.04 (1.1), and on anyone a statute entitles to be heard on the appeal. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 10.6 | With it, you must serve and file an appellant's certificate respecting evidence (Form 61C), with proof of service. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.9 | You must file the notice of appeal, with proof of service, in the Registrar's office within ten days after service. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.9 | It also includes an appeal only about costs that were in the discretion of the court that made the costs order. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.8 | For a Superior Court judge's final costs order, leave to appeal to the Divisional Court must come from a panel of that court. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 10 | You must serve a notice of motion for leave within 15 days after the order is made, unless a statute provides otherwise. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.5 | You must then file it, with proof of service, in the Registrar's office within five days after service. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.1 | If leave is granted, you must deliver the notice of appeal within seven days after leave is granted. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 13.1 | For the Divisional Court, deliver the appellant's certificate respecting evidence with it in that time. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 11.1 | Then you ask for leave to appeal the costs in the notice of appeal, or in a supplementary notice of appeal, as part of the relief sought. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.3 | The panel hearing the other appeal decides on leave, and if leave is granted, it may then hear the costs appeal. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.7 | An order made under the Divorce Act cannot be appealed more than thirty days after the day it was made. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.1 | A judgment granting a divorce cannot be appealed on or after the day the divorce takes effect. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 10.7 | Subject to the rest of section 12 of the Divorce Act, a divorce takes effect on the thirty-first day after the day the judgment is given. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 9.1 | Apart from an appeal, the court may, on motion, change an order that was obtained by fraud or that contains a mistake. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.1 | It also applies to an order made with notice, if an affected party was not there when it was made. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.1 | That is when the notice was inadequate, or the party could not be there for a reason the court accepts. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.4 | Rule 14, the rule on motions, applies to that kind of motion, with necessary changes. |
| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 8.5 | Under rule 15, a party who wants a final order changed must serve and file a motion to change (Form 15). |

| family:both:final-order-made | What to do next | 11.2 | For orders under the Child, Youth and Family Services Act, 2017, rule 15 covers only a final order made under section 102 of that Act. |

| family:both:appealing-temporary-order | Your deadline | 9.1 | A temporary order made by a judge of the Superior Court of Justice is different. |

| family:both:appealing-temporary-order | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |

| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 9.7 | In a joint application, the divorce is dealt with on affidavit evidence. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 8.1 | It is also dealt with that way if the respondent files no answer, or files one and then withdraws it. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 11.6 | An Ontario court can hear a divorce if either spouse has habitually lived in Ontario for at least one year right before the case started. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 8.7 | If so, the spouses must have lived separate and apart for at least one year before the divorce is decided. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 10 | Living together again for up to 90 days in total, mainly to try to reconcile, does not by itself break that year. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 11.1 | Or it means physical or mental cruelty by that spouse since the marriage, if it makes living together intolerable. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 10.6 | If the application claims adultery with another person, that person does not need to be named. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What is happening | 8.5 | If named, that person must be served with the application and has all the rights of a respondent. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 12.6 | A marriage registration certificate can be filed instead. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 8.4 | This is not needed if the application says it is impractical to get one and explains why. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 13.7 | It is issued under the Central Registry of Divorce Proceedings Regulations (Canada). |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 8.4 | You must also file an affidavit for divorce in Form 36. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.8 | You file it as the applicant if the respondent files no answer, or files one and later withdraws it. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 8.2 | In a joint application, the applicants file it. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 11.2 | The affidavit must confirm that all the information in the application is correct, except as it says. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 8.5 | If neither kind of marriage certificate has been filed, it must give enough facts to prove the marriage. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.5 | If the marriage did not take place in Canada, it must give proof of any earlier divorce, or of the death of a party's earlier spouse. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.1 | It must describe the support arrangements for any children of the marriage, as the Divorce Act requires. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.8 | The income and financial facts that section 21 of the child support guidelines requires must be attached. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.1 | You must also file a stamped envelope addressed to each party. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.5 | If the divorce order is to include a support order, you must file one more copy of the draft divorce order. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 12.2 | The clerk files that copy with the Director of the Family Responsibility Office. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What to do next | 9.9 | If no answer is filed, the clerk must, on request, prepare the documents for a judge in an uncontested divorce. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 10.4 | The court must be satisfied that reasonable arrangements have been made to support any children of the marriage. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 9.8 | The court must also satisfy itself that there has been no collusion in the divorce application. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 8.8 | If it finds collusion in presenting the application, it must dismiss it. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 10.7 | Under the Act, collusion is an agreement or plot to subvert the administration of justice. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 8.4 | An applicant may be part of it directly or indirectly. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 9.9 | An agreement is not collusion as far as it deals with separation or financial support. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 15.6 | The same goes for division of property, parenting time or decision-making responsibility. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 8.2 | Some divorces are based on adultery or cruelty. |
| family:applicant:uncontested-or-joint-divorce | What happens after | 10.5 | If that spouse condoned or connived at the act or conduct complained of, the court must dismiss the application. |

| family:both:notice-of-approaching-dismissal | What happens after | 10.2 | If no step is taken in time, the clerk dismisses the case by preparing and signing an order, with no costs payable by any party. |
| family:both:notice-of-approaching-dismissal | What happens after | 9.3 | If the clerk dismissed the case because no step was taken within the 60 days, a judge may, on motion, set aside that order. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 10.5 | The clerk issues the notice (Form 30) when the party enforcing the order files a statement of money owed. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 10.2 | If the recipient is enforcing, the recipient also files a request (Form 30A). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 14.4 | The Director of the Family Responsibility Office may be enforcing instead. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 9.4 | If so, the rules on enforcement by the recipient apply to the Director. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 11 | When the payor could face jail, the notice may be served only by leaving a copy with the payor, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What is happening | 13.4 | That does not apply if the court orders otherwise, or if the party serving is the Director of the Family Responsibility Office. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 9.4 | That party is the recipient, or the Director if the Director is enforcing. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 9.3 | If the recipient is enforcing, the Act says the financial statement must be in the form the court rules set. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 11.1 | If the Director is enforcing, its notice may ask you to give the Director a financial statement and proof of income. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 8.5 | You are presumed to admit that the statement of money owed is correct. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 10.4 | If you do not file the financial statement or do not appear, the court may issue a warrant for your arrest to bring you to court. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What to do next | 9.8 | If you are the recipient enforcing the order, you must serve and file a new statement of money owed. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 11.2 | If you are the payor, serve a financial statement and a default dispute (Form 30B) on the party enforcing the order, and file them. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 14.3 | The party enforcing is the recipient, or the Director of the Family Responsibility Office. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 8.4 | Use Form 13 for the financial statement. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 9.1 | If the Director is enforcing, use the form the regulations require, as the notice will say. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 8.5 | You are presumed to agree that the statement of money owed is correct. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 9.1 | That changes only if you file a default dispute saying it is wrong and giving detailed reasons. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 8.9 | If you do not file the financial statement or do not come, the court can issue a warrant for your arrest. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 9.5 | Unless the court finds you cannot pay for valid reasons, it can order payments, security, or jail for up to 180 days. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 12.1 | If you are the recipient enforcing the order, serve and file a new statement of money owed no more than 7 days before the default hearing. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 12.9 | If the Director of the Family Responsibility Office is enforcing, the Director must do this only in two cases. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What happens after | 9.9 | Unless shown otherwise, the payor is presumed able to pay the arrears and later payments. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What happens after | 13.5 | A statement of arrears the Director prepared and served is also presumed correct for arrears that built up while the order was filed with it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What happens after | 8.4 | The court may make orders unless it is satisfied the payor cannot pay for valid reasons. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What happens after | 8.5 | These orders include paying all or part of the arrears, and giving security. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-default-hearing | What happens after | 13.4 | If you do not obey a condition, the recipient may bring a motion, served on you by special service, and the court may issue a warrant of committal against you. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What is happening | 8.3 | A contempt motion enforces an order that is not a payment order, in the same case the order was made in. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What is happening | 14 | Unless the court orders otherwise, the notice of contempt motion (Form 31) must be served together with a supporting affidavit, by special service. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What is happening | 8.7 | Unless the court orders otherwise, it must be served by leaving a copy with you, as rule 6 (3) (a) sets out. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What to do next | 9.8 | Unless the court orders otherwise, you, as the party responding, must serve all your evidence in response. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What to do next | 10.5 | After that, the party making the motion may serve evidence that replies to any new matters raised by your evidence. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What to do next | 9.1 | Unless the court orders otherwise, no other evidence may be used. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What to do next | 10.5 | Any affidavit on this motion, including yours, must as much as possible hold only what the signer knows first-hand. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What to do next | 10.1 | For a contempt motion, the information must also not be expected to be disputed. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | Your deadline | 8.4 | The general rule for responding to a motion sets 4 days before the motion date. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | Your deadline | 9.4 | Under the motion rules, the party responding serves all their evidence in response. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What happens after | 8.9 | Or it may order you to do anything else it decides, not do what it forbids, or obey any other order. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What happens after | 10.6 | The court may also give permission to issue a writ of temporary seizure (Form 28C) against your property. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What happens after | 13.1 | The third is s. 53 of the Family Responsibility and Support Arrears Enforcement Act, 1996. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What happens after | 9.1 | A contempt order for jail or a fine may be suspended (paused) on appropriate conditions. |
| family:both:served-with-contempt-motion | What happens after | 10.6 | A party may later say, in a Form 32C affidavit on a motion with special service, that you broke such a condition. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What is happening | 11.2 | Another party has asked you for an affidavit listing your documents. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 8.5 | Within 10 days after the request, you must give the other party that affidavit. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 9.1 | Papers that must be served under rule 13 (financial disclosure) are left out of this 10-day duty. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 8.4 | The duty is also not on the Office of the Children’s Lawyer or on children’s aid societies. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 9.3 | If you claim a document is protected by legal privilege, the court may, on motion, look at it and decide. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 10.8 | One is if the other party was allowed to see it and given a free copy at least 30 days before the settlement conference. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 8.5 | If so, you must immediately serve a new affidavit with the correct list. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 14.7 | If you do not follow this rule or an order under it, the court may order you to give the affidavit, let the other party examine a document, or give them a free copy. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 9.1 | It may also order that a document that helps your case cannot be used without its permission. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 9.8 | Those orders are in addition to the court’s general powers when the rules or an order are not followed. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What to do next | 10.6 | They include an order for costs, a penalty or fine, dismissing a claim, or striking out a document a party filed. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | Your deadline | 11.8 | Give the other party an affidavit listing every document that is relevant to any issue in the case. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | Your deadline | 10.4 | The other party can then ask to see a listed document, unless it is protected by legal privilege. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What happens after | 12.8 | On request, the other party is entitled to examine any document in your list, unless it is protected by legal privilege. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What happens after | 14 | For a document listed in a corrected affidavit, they are entitled to examine it and get a copy free of charge, unless it is privileged. |
| family:both:document-disclosure-requested | What happens after | 14.4 | The same right to examine and get a copy applies to any document mentioned in your application, answer, reply, notice of motion or affidavit. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What is happening | 8.5 | An offer is an offer to settle one or more claims in a case, motion, appeal or enforcement. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 8.9 | A party may act in person, without a representative. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 12 | If the party making the offer has a licensed representative, the representative must sign it too. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 14.4 | A licensed representative is a lawyer or family legal services provider. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 10.2 | The party who made an offer may withdraw it at any time before it is accepted, by serving a notice of withdrawal. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 9.7 | This must be done before the offer is withdrawn, and before the court begins to give a decision on a claim the offer deals with. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 9.1 | You may accept an offer even if you rejected it before or made a counter-offer. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 9.1 | The terms of an offer must not be mentioned in any document filed in the continuing record. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 8.8 | They also must not be mentioned to the judge hearing the claim until the judge has dealt with all the issues except costs. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What to do next | 8.7 | But an acceptance of their offer, or their acceptance of an offer, does not bind them until the court approves. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 8.8 | Do it before the offer is withdrawn or ends, and before the court starts to give a decision on the claim it covers. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 9.5 | The party who made it can withdraw it at any time before it is accepted, by serving a notice of withdrawal. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 9.4 | It can still be accepted after it was rejected, or after a counter-offer. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 10.7 | Making, withdrawing, accepting or rejecting an offer can affect costs. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 17.4 | If all of these conditions are met, the party who made the offer is entitled, unless the court orders otherwise, to costs up to the date the offer was served and full recovery of costs from that date to the end of that step. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | Your deadline | 8.9 | Even an offer that does not meet these conditions may still be considered when the court sets the amount of costs. |
| family:both:offer-to-settle | What happens after | 11.7 | Making, withdrawing, accepting and rejecting offers can have costs results under rule 24. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What is happening | 11.2 | After an answer is filed, an applicant can amend without the court's permission in one way, except in a child protection case. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What is happening | 9.9 | They serve and file the amended application and also file the consent of all parties. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 11.1 | To do so, serve and file an amended answer within 14 days after you were served with the amended application. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 13.9 | In a child protection case, a different rule applies if something significant about the child changed after the first document was filed. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 12.6 | An application for an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18 is an exception to that rule. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 8.3 | Also note in the margin of each changed page the rule or order that allows the change and the date of the change. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 13.9 | Your amended answer may add a claim about decision-making responsibility, parenting time or contact with a child that was not in your first answer. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 10.7 | They are a claim for support, a property claim, or a claim for exclusive possession of the matrimonial home and its contents. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 14.6 | If the amendment adds one of these claims against you, you must serve and file a financial statement within the time for serving and filing an answer, even if you do not serve an answer. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What to do next | 9.9 | If your amended answer adds one of these claims, you must usually serve and file a financial statement with it. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 9.1 | If you want to amend your answer without the court's permission, serve and file an amended answer. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 9.1 | In a child protection case, if something significant about the child has changed, the time is 30 days. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 11.5 | After the time runs out, you need the court's permission on a motion to amend your answer, unless the parties agree in writing to a later time. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 14 | Your amended answer may add a claim about decision-making responsibility, parenting time or contact that was not in your first answer. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 11.8 | The amendment may add a claim for support, a property claim, or a claim for exclusive possession of the matrimonial home against you. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:respondent:served-with-amended-application | What happens after | 8.8 | The court must give it, unless the change would harm another party in a way that costs or an adjournment could not make up for. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What is happening | 10.5 | The other party is appealing an order of the Ontario Court of Justice to the Superior Court of Justice. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 9.8 | The appellant must decide, after talking with you, whether the appeal needs a transcript of the evidence. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 10.6 | If you both agree on what evidence to transcribe, the appellant must order that evidence transcribed. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 12.4 | If you cannot agree, the appellant must order a transcript of all the oral evidence from the hearing, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 14.2 | Within 60 days after the appellant serves its appeal record and factum, you must serve your respondent's factum on the appellant and anyone else entitled to be heard, and file it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 10.4 | That shorter time does not apply to an appeal of an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 9.2 | Some material from the first court may be needed for the appeal but missing from the appellant's appeal record. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 9.6 | If so, you must also serve and file, in the same time, a respondent's appeal record with a copy of that material. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 10.2 | Its parts include your position on each issue the appellant raised and the exact order you ask the appeal court to make. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 13.6 | Your notice of appeal must be served on every other party affected by the appeal or entitled to appeal, and on the clerk of the court where the order was made. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 12.3 | In those, also serve every other person entitled to notice under subsection 79 (3) of that Act who appeared at the hearing. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 10.8 | It must be served within 30 days after the date of a final order, or seven days after the date of a temporary order. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 8.6 | In a case under the Child, Youth and Family Services Act, 2017, it is 30 days for a temporary order too. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 10.5 | Again, that does not apply to an appeal of an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 11.1 | If a transcript is needed, the appellant must file proof it was ordered within 30 days after filing the notice of appeal. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What to do next | 8.7 | Sometimes the appellant does not do that, or does not serve and file its appeal record and factum in time. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 9.1 | Serve your respondent's factum on the appellant and anyone else entitled to be heard, and file it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 8.8 | The appellant decides whether a transcript is needed after talking with you. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 9.1 | It may not file proof that a needed transcript was ordered within 30 days after filing the notice. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What happens after | 11.8 | When the appellant's record and factum are filed, and yours are filed or your time to file has run out, the clerk must schedule the appeal for hearing. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What happens after | 9.8 | It does stay (pause), until the appeal is decided, any other payment order in the order under appeal. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What happens after | 11.7 | The appellant may withdraw the appeal by serving a notice of withdrawal (Form 12) on every other party and filing it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-appeal | What happens after | 15.5 | In an appeal of an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18, the order is stayed (paused) until the appeal is decided, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What is happening | 10.6 | A support order filed in the Director's office is in default, and the Director has served you with a first notice. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What is happening | 8.6 | It says your driver's licence may be suspended unless you act within 30 days after the day it was served. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What is happening | 10 | If you do not act in that time, the Director may direct the Registrar of Motor Vehicles to suspend your licence. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What is happening | 9.1 | An order to refrain is a court order that the Director not direct the suspension of your licence. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 9.1 | You can make an arrangement the Director accepts for paying the support and the arrears. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 10.9 | If you bring a motion to change the support order, you may also bring a motion for an order to refrain, on notice to the Director. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 12.1 | You may bring the motion to refrain first if you or your lawyer undertakes to get a court date for the motion to change within 20 days after the order to refrain. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 9.9 | If no court date is obtained within those 20 days, the order to refrain ends automatically. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 9 | You do not need a motion to change if you have started an appeal of the support order and it has not been decided. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 18.5 | Submitting a support variation application to the designated authority in Ontario under the Interjurisdictional Support Orders Act, 2002 counts as a motion to change. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 17.1 | So does submitting one to the Central Authority in Ontario under the International Recovery of Child Support and Family Maintenance Convention Act, 2023. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 16.1 | With the notice of motion, you must serve and file a financial statement (Form 13 or 13.1, or Form 4 under Ontario Regulation 167/97) and the proof of income set out in section 15 of that regulation. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What to do next | 14.6 | If the proof of income is not served and filed within the 20 days, the court may, on the Director's motion, change or end the order to refrain without proof of a material change in circumstances. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | Your deadline | 9.4 | Make an arrangement the Director accepts for paying the support and the arrears. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | Your deadline | 9 | To do that, you or your lawyer must give an undertaking (a formal promise) to get a court date for the motion to change. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | Your deadline | 8.9 | You do not need a motion to change if you have appealed the support order and the appeal is not decided. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | Your deadline | 8.4 | Serve and file a financial statement and proof of income with the notice of motion. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | Your deadline | 11.8 | A motion must normally be served at least 6 days before it is heard, not counting Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What happens after | 11.1 | A court may make an order to refrain only within the 30 days in the first notice, which cannot be extended, and only one for each first notice. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What happens after | 8.3 | If the 30th day is a day court offices are closed, the order must be made by the last day they are open before then. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What happens after | 10.8 | The court must file a copy in the Director's office promptly after it is signed, and it takes effect only when filed there. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What happens after | 10.9 | It ends on the earliest of several days, which include six months after it is made and the day the motion to change or the appeal is decided. |
| family:both:served-with-licence-suspension-notice | What happens after | 10.3 | Payment terms in an order to refrain do not stop arrears from adding up or stop other ways of enforcing the support order. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 8.4 | A payment order can be enforced in several ways, and garnishment is one of them. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 9.9 | It also covers money that becomes payable by the garnishee to the payor after that. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 12.9 | If the garnishee is a financial institution served as rule 29 requires, the notice reaches only accounts that were open when it was served. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 10.5 | The notice stays in effect until it is withdrawn or stopped under rule 29, or until the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 10.4 | It keeps covering future periodic payments. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 9.1 | The notice can also cover a debt owed to the payor and another person jointly. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What is happening | 10.7 | The garnishee must also immediately send the other person a notice to co-owner of debt (Form 29C). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What to do next | 11.8 | Those 10 days run from service of the notice of garnishment or of a statutory declaration of indexed support. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What to do next | 11.2 | The Director or clerk must pay out money received immediately, even if a dispute has been filed. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What to do next | 8.9 | The court may order that this does not apply, at a garnishment hearing or on a motion to change the garnishment. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What to do next | 8.3 | And once served with the notice to co-owner of debt, the sheriff, clerk or Director must hold the money for 30 days. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 13.4 | If you are the payor or the garnishee, you can dispute a notice of garnishment or a statutory declaration of indexed support. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 12.3 | You have 10 days after the day you were served with the notice of garnishment or the statutory declaration of indexed support. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 9.2 | Except for a joint debt, money taken under the garnishment is paid out even if a dispute is filed. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 9.8 | After a dispute is served and filed, the clerk issues a notice of garnishment hearing if asked. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 8.4 | The payor's situation may later change in a way that affects their ability to pay. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 9.5 | While the garnishment is in place, the payor may go back to work for the garnishee or start getting money from a new source. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What happens after | 10.5 | Within 10 days after a dispute is served and filed, the clerk must issue a notice of garnishment hearing (Form 29H) on request. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What happens after | 8.5 | These include an order dismissing the dispute or changing how much is garnished. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What happens after | 10.4 | They also include an order suspending the garnishment or setting aside the notice. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What happens after | 10.4 | If the payor's situation changes in a way that affects their ability to pay, the court may, on a motion, make the same kinds of orders. |
| family:both:served-with-notice-of-garnishment | What happens after | 9.5 | If you are the payor and you go back to work for or get money again from the garnishee, you must send a notice within 10 days. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What is happening | 11.3 | The court may stay or dismiss a case that appears on its face to be frivolous or vexatious or otherwise an abuse of the court process. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What is happening | 8.3 | When the court decides such an order may be appropriate, it has the clerk give the parties a notice in Form 1.4A. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What is happening | 9.9 | If the notice is about a case, the case is automatically stayed once the clerk gives notice to any party. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What is happening | 8.6 | It stays that way until the court makes an order staying or dismissing it, or an order declining to. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What is happening | 9.1 | Until then, no party may take any step in the case other than the steps in rule 1.4, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What to do next | 10.7 | If you brought the case, you may file a written submission responding to the notice, no later than 15 days after receiving it. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What to do next | 8.7 | If no submission is filed in time, the court may make the order without any further notice to the parties. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What to do next | 13 | If you are another party and you receive a copy, you may file a responding submission of no more than 10 pages, no later than 10 days after receiving it. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What to do next | 11.1 | The notice and any copy of a submission are given by regular service, and rule 6 decides when they count as received. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | Your deadline | 10.5 | If you brought the case or motion the notice is about, file a written submission of no more than 10 pages responding to the notice. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | Your deadline | 10.9 | If you are another party and the clerk gives you a copy of that submission, you may file a reply submission of no more than 10 pages. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |

| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What happens after | 8.4 | For a case, it either makes an order staying or dismissing it, or an order declining to. |
| family:both:notice-case-may-be-stayed-or-dismissed | What happens after | 11.8 | If the court stays or dismisses a motion, it may also order that the party who made it may not make other motions in the case without the court's permission. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What is happening | 12.1 | A copy of each document named in it must be attached, unless you already have a copy or it is not practical to attach one. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 10.9 | If you do not serve a response (Form 22A) within 20 days, you are treated as admitting, for this case only, that the facts are true and the documents are genuine. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 10.5 | In your response, you can deny that a particular fact is true or that a particular document is genuine. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 9.9 | Or you can refuse to admit a particular fact or document, but you must give your reasons for each refusal. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 10.2 | Any fact or document your response does not deny, or refuse to admit with reasons, is treated as admitted for this case. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 9.3 | Admitting that a document is genuine has a set meaning, which depends on what the document is said to be. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 9.1 | For an original, it means the document was written, signed or sealed as it appears to have been. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What to do next | 17.2 | For a copy of something usually sent from one person to another, like a letter, fax or electronic message, it also means it was sent as it appears and was received by the person it is addressed to. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | Your deadline | 8.4 | You can withdraw an admission only if the other party agrees or the court allows it. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-request-to-admit | What happens after | 9.1 | An admission can be taken back only if the other party consents or the court gives permission. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What is happening | 13.7 | The Children's Lawyer is looking into decision-making responsibility, parenting time or contact for a child in your case. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What is happening | 16.7 | These rights cover the child's decision-making responsibility, parenting time, contact, support or education. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What is happening | 8.4 | The Children's Lawyer may report and make recommendations to the court on the results. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What to do next | 9.1 | Once you are served with the notice, you must serve the Children's Lawyer with every paper in the case about the child. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What to do next | 18.6 | That means papers about decision-making responsibility, parenting time, contact, support or education. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What to do next | 9.2 | Within 90 days after serving the notice, the Children's Lawyer must serve a report on the parties and file it. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What to do next | 9.2 | That person must serve the affidavit on the parties, with the report attached, and file both with the court. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What to do next | 8.4 | If you dispute anything in the report, you may serve and file a statement saying so. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 10.6 | If you dispute anything in the Children's Lawyer's report, serve and file a statement disputing it. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 10 | From the day you were served with the Children's Lawyer's notice, serve the Children's Lawyer with every paper about the child. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 18.6 | That means papers about decision-making responsibility, parenting time, contact, support or education. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What happens after | 9.5 | The trial may be held only after those 30 days end, or after the parties file a statement giving up their right to that time. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What happens after | 8.5 | The filed affidavit and report form part of the evidence at the hearing. |
| family:both:childrens-lawyer-report | What happens after | 16.8 | If a party disputes the facts in the report, the Children's Lawyer must, if the court directs it, attend the hearing on behalf of the child and have the person who did the investigation or meeting attend as a witness. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What is happening | 10.7 | A recipient may ask for a financial statement only once in six months, unless the court gives permission to ask more often. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What to do next | 11.1 | Within 15 days after you were served with the request, you must send a completed financial statement (Form 13) to the recipient. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What to do next | 8.8 | Some parts of rule 13 also apply to your statement, with necessary changes. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What to do next | 17.2 | Under the rule on insufficient information, if the recipient believes your statement does not give enough information to fully understand your finances, they must ask you in writing for what is needed. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What to do next | 13.6 | If you do not give the requested information within seven days of the request, the court may order you to give it or to serve and file a new financial statement. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | Your deadline | 9.4 | Send a completed financial statement (Form 13) to the recipient by mail, fax or email. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | Your deadline | 8.4 | If the court has ordered you to serve and file a financial statement, serve and file it. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
