| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What happens after | 9.5 | If you do not serve and file it within 10 days after being served with that order, the court may order you jailed for up to 40 days. |
| family:both:served-with-request-for-financial-statement | What happens after | 9.1 | That can only happen on a motion served on you by special service, and the jail time may be all at once or on and off. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 10.9 | The purpose of this hearing is to let a judge, in a single hearing, help the parties settle issues and decide issues that are not settled. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 16.1 | It is only available in the Superior Court of Justice, including its Family Court, in a municipality shown on the Ontario Courts website as chosen by the Chief Justice. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 15.4 | It does not apply to cases under the Child, Youth and Family Services Act, 2017, interjurisdictional support cases (rule 37) or international child abduction cases (rule 37.2). |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 12.3 | First, the case has no major credibility issues that need cross-examination. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 9.8 | Third, it is reasonable to expect the issues can be settled or decided in a summary way. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What is happening | 9.8 | If those conditions are not met, the court must not order the hearing, unless it decides the hearing is right in the circumstances. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 8.8 | To ask for the hearing, each party must serve and file a request and consent form (Form 43 or 43A) and a motion form (Form 14B). |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 13 | If you are serving and filing papers for a conference or other hearing, you may file the request and consent form with those papers instead of with a motion form. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 9.6 | For the hearing, each party must serve and file a set of papers by the times below, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 8.4 | They include an affidavit (Form 43B or 14A) of no more than 12 pages, not counting exhibits. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 10.5 | The exhibits can total no more than 10 pages, not counting the other listed papers, unless the court orders otherwise. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 10.7 | They also include any relevant agreements, minutes of settlement or court orders. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 10.2 | One example is an updated financial statement for a support claim. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 9.3 | The applicant, or the party who made a motion to change, must serve and file them no later than 20 days before the hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 10.3 | The respondent, or the party responding to a motion to change, must serve and file them no later than 10 days before the hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 12.6 | No later than five days before the hearing, the applicant or the party who made the motion may serve and file a reply affidavit (Form 14A) of no more than four pages. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 9.3 | The reply may only deal with new matters raised in the other party's affidavit. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 8.9 | The parts of an affidavit you fill in must be typed or written clearly, double spaced, in at least 12 point size. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 11.1 | Each party must give a copy of a confirmation (Form 43C) to every other party by mail, fax, email or any other method. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 15 | You can deliver it to the court office, email it to the court office, or submit it through Justice Services Online or the Ontario Courts Public Portal, whichever is available. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What to do next | 13.5 | In the confirmation, you must say if you are satisfied that the other party's disclosure is enough for the issues to be settled or decided at the hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.8 | If you are the applicant, or the party who made a motion to change, serve and file your hearing papers, as listed below. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.1 | Do this at least 20 days before the date of the binding judicial dispute resolution hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 10.2 | Any relevant agreements, minutes of settlement or court orders. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 14 | For a support claim, an updated financial statement and certificate of financial disclosure (Form 13A). |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 12.3 | For property issues, any supporting papers and expert or professional reports. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 11.1 | For parenting issues, any expert or professional reports. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 10.4 | The exception is with every other party's written consent or the court's permission. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 11 | If you are the respondent, or the party responding to a motion to change, serve and file the same hearing papers, as listed above. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.1 | Do this at least 10 days before the date of the binding judicial dispute resolution hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 15 | If you are the applicant or the party who made the motion, you may serve and file a reply affidavit of no more than four pages about new matters in the other party's affidavit. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.1 | Do this at least 5 days before the date of the binding judicial dispute resolution hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.6 | Give a copy of the confirmation (Form 43C) to every other party. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 9.1 | Do this at least 3 days before the date of the binding judicial dispute resolution hearing. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |

| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What happens after | 8.4 | It starts with settlement talks, with the judge helping the parties settle issues by agreement. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What happens after | 8.7 | To decide an issue, the judge may ask a party or witness more questions, and a party may suggest questions. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What happens after | 8.3 | If the judge decides more talks are not expected to settle any more issues, the judge may decide any issue left. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What happens after | 9.1 | That decision may be temporary or final. |
| family:both:binding-judicial-dispute-resolution | What happens after | 10.7 | If a party does not take part, the judge may hold the hearing based only on the evidence filed for or received at the hearing. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 9.7 | Questioning means asking a person questions out loud, under oath or affirmation. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 11 | In a case that is not a child protection case, a party can get information from another party about any issue in two ways. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 10 | Outside a child protection case, if you do not consent to be questioned, the party who wants to question you needs a court order. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 10.5 | In a child protection case, a party has a right to get information from another party about any issue. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 8.5 | To question the other party, the party must serve them with a summons to witness (Form 23) by special service. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 9.5 | Unless the court orders otherwise, it must be served by leaving a copy with the person, as rule 6 (3) (a) sets out. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 11.3 | It is an application for an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18. |
| family:both:questioning | What is happening | 14.1 | For that application, the consent-or-order rule applies instead. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 12.4 | The court may, on motion, order a person to be questioned by a party, or to give information by affidavit or another way. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 11.9 | Second, the information is not easily available any other way. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 8.4 | Third, it will not cause unacceptable delay or undue expense. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 8.5 | An order can affect someone who is not a party only if they were served by special service. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 14 | If you want to question someone, you must first have served and filed any answer, financial statement or net family property statement the rules require. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 8.5 | The exception is papers in reply to the answers or information you get. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 13.4 | A party can be questioned on their financial statement only after a written request for more information has been made under rule 13 (11) (a). |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 10.1 | The questioning takes place in the municipality where the person to be questioned lives. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 10.2 | They may not agree on the date and time, who records the questioning, how it is recorded, or paying a non-party's expenses. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 10.5 | Unless the court orders otherwise, a person being questioned may be asked about any matter in dispute in the case. |
| family:both:questioning | What to do next | 16 | They may also be asked for the names of people who might know about the claims, the witnesses and expert witnesses a party plans to call, and, if relevant, any insurance policy that may pay part of an order. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 10.7 | If you are arranging the questioning, serve every party with notice of who will be questioned, and the address, date and time. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 14.4 | Before you can question anyone, you must have served and filed any answer, financial statement or net family property statement the rules require. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 8.1 | You must also promise in writing not to serve or file more papers for the next step, except in reply. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 8.7 | The questioning takes place in the city or town where the person lives, unless they and you agree on another. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 12.3 | To question someone on their financial statement, you must first have asked in writing for the missing information under r. 13 (11) (a). |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:questioning | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 10.6 | If a person being questioned refuses to answer a question, the court may, on motion, decide whether the question is proper. |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 9.3 | Sometimes the person who refused is a party, or is questioned for a party. |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 11.5 | Then that party cannot use the refused information as evidence unless the court gives permission. |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 12.8 | The court must give that permission unless using the information would harm another party or cause an unacceptable delay in the trial. |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 10.7 | It may attach conditions, including an adjournment. |
| family:both:questioning | What happens after | 10.6 | If so, you must give the correct and complete information in writing to all parties immediately. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What is happening | 9.8 | A party who does not want to go on with all or part of a case may withdraw all or part of their application, answer or reply. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What to do next | 9.3 | To withdraw, serve a notice of withdrawal (Form 12) on every other party and file it. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What to do next | 9.8 | Their application, answer or reply, in whole or in part, can be withdrawn only with the court's permission. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What to do next | 9.4 | The notice of motion for permission must be served on every other party. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What to do next | 10.5 | It must also be served on the Public Guardian and Trustee for a special party, or on the Children's Lawyer for a child party. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What happens after | 9.8 | If you withdraw all or part of an application, answer or reply, you must pay every other party's costs. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What happens after | 9.7 | This applies unless the court orders otherwise or the parties agree otherwise. |
| family:both:withdrawing-a-claim | What happens after | 8.4 | If the party withdrawing is a government agency, costs are up to the court instead. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What is happening | 9.7 | The rules let an applicant amend the application. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 8.5 | To do so, serve and file an amended application the same way you start a case under rule 8. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 8.3 | The court must give it, unless the change would harm another party in a way that costs or a delay could not make up for. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 10.7 | In your amended application, underline all changes. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 8.3 | Also note in the margin of each changed page the rule or order that allows the change and the date of the change. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 13.1 | A claim about a child here means one about decision-making responsibility, parenting time or contact. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 8.4 | These include an affidavit in Form 35.1. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 9.8 | It applies if a significant change about the child happens after the first document is filed. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 9.8 | Then the applicant may serve and file an amended application, an amended plan of care, or both. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What to do next | 12.6 | An application for an order under sections 53.1 to 53.5 of Ontario Regulation 155/18 is an exception to that rule. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What happens after | 14.7 | Under rule 8, an application must be served immediately on every other party. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What happens after | 8.8 | Once served, the respondent may amend their answer without the court's permission. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What happens after | 10.5 | In the child protection case described above, the respondent may serve and file an amended answer and plan of care. |
| family:applicant:amending-own-application | What happens after | 9.3 | They have 30 days after being served if served in Canada or the United States, or 60 days if served outside them. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What is happening | 11.9 | The recipient can serve an appointment for a financial examination (Form 27C) when a payment order is in default. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What is happening | 12.3 | A recipient may hold only one financial examination of you in a six-month period, unless the court gives permission for more. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What is happening | 8.4 | At the examination, you may be asked about many things. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What is happening | 10.5 | You may also be asked about your ability to pay under the order, and whether you intend to obey it. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What to do next | 10.7 | The appointment requires you to come to the financial examination. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What to do next | 8.4 | You must also serve a financial statement (Form 13) on the recipient. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | Your deadline | 9.1 | Serve a financial statement (Form 13) on the recipient. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | Your deadline | 13.6 | Bring any document or thing named in the appointment that you control or can get, that relates to enforcing the order, and that is not protected by legal privilege. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | Your deadline | 8.3 | If the last day of a period falls on a day court offices are closed, the period ends on the next day they are open (r. 3 (3)). |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | Your deadline | 12.9 | For a time that must be met a number of days before a date, the rules do not clearly say how this applies, so the safe course is to act by the last open day before it. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | Your deadline | 9.6 | For a period of less than seven days, Saturdays, Sundays and other days when all court offices are closed are not counted (r. 3 (2)). |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What happens after | 10.1 | Rule 27 (19) applies if you miss the examination or fail to serve the financial statement. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What happens after | 10.7 | Then the court may, on motion, order another examination and give directions. |
| family:both:served-with-financial-examination-appointment | What happens after | 10.8 | If you then fail, without sufficient excuse, to obey that order or direction, the court may act on a motion with special service. |
